# ▣ THE REGISTER — the corpus's open questions, opened r3009

***`PROTECTED_OPEN` closed at r3001 with fifteen rows struck.*** **The live set is not fixed here and is not to be read from this line: `THE_FRONTIER` is generated from the rows below and is the authority on which are open.** *A masthead that names its own live rows goes stale the first time one is struck — this one named `PO-13`, `PO-14` and `PO-15` and two of those three were struck in this same file.*

**⚙ THE RULES, CARRIED FORWARD UNCHANGED:** *a row is struck when **its OBJECT is answered and the answer is receipted** — not when it is convenient, and never on a person-gate. **Read the row's own history, then the closures, then the receipts** (§⑤·8). **Calibrate a detector against known instances before believing a zero** (§⑤·6). **A closure's consequence does not have to name a row to reach it** (§⑤·7). **An audit must normalise the source the way the extraction did** (§⑤·9).*

⚠ ***AND THE LESSON THAT COST A HUNDRED TURNS: for eighty revisions the answer to "has this been done?" was usually YES, and the register did not know it. **Look before declaring a build.** These have been checked against that — but check again.***

## ⛭⛭ THE ROOMS — *which rows share a wall, and where each room is open (`r6443`)*

***The completeness test below asks whether every family has a row. This asks a different question of the
same nine: which rows discharge TOGETHER.*** *A row is a hole in a wall; a room closes when its walls meet.
**Grouped by shared discharge, not by sector** --- the sector headings in `THE_FRONTIER` are the generated
cut and are not this one.*

| room | rows | where it is open |
|---|---|---|
| **① the counterterm ledger** | `PO-23`, `PO-43` | **one**: the interacting tower's ultraviolet definition --- *`P07`'s last of three, and **not reachable by refining the free-spectrum method***. ⌗ *The entropy wall is gone at `r6471`: the ledger does not count a counterterm coefficient, so the branch is costless to it and was never a wall of this room* |
| **② the matter content, and the head** | `PO-30`, `PO-31` *(with struck `PO-26`, `PO-40`, `PO-41`)* | **one, with a direction** *(`r6479`)*: a generative law for the **content**. ***`PO-31`'s opening is the shadow of it*** --- every lap after the first inherits its cut's shape and **the head inherits nothing**, so the law that fixes a cut absent a progenitor is what would say what matter is on the head's collapse leg. **`PO-30` discharged discharges both; `PO-31` alone moves neither** |

| **③ the acoustic front and the interior** | `PO-13`, `PO-24`, `PO-25` | **one** *(was two; `60`'s `r6522` ran the joint fit)*: `PO-25`'s condition, $m(r)=o(1/r)$ at the origin. ⚠ *And `r6525` finds it is **not a reading**: $m(r)$ near the origin is set by the matter, and `P08` supplies the dynamics **given content** while not supplying the constitutive relation. **It awaits an interior evolution with chosen matter --- the same thing `PO-31`'s remainder awaits, in its own words "a modelling task awaiting a progenitor interior".*** ⇒ **`PO-30` wants a LAW; these two want a MODEL, so they are not downstream of it --- and `PO-30` stays the only one of the three answerable without first choosing something arbitrary** \rcpt{P03_the_last_two_openings_await_the_same_interior} |
| **④ --- ** | `PO-36` | **one**, and *it does not discriminate this framework* --- see below |

**⛭ ① IS THE CLOSEST TO CLOSING, AND ONE OF ITS WALLS WAS FOUND ONLY AT `r6437`.** *Both rows now carry
values --- the log on the degenerate combination, the Weyl-squared coefficient off it. `PO-43`'s two
replacement questions resolved to one answered and one **not independent**: a topological term contributes
no field equation, so its coefficient's only home is the horizon entropy. ⇒ ***That wall is shared with
`PO-32`, which is STRUCK*** --- so a room of the quantum sector is bounded in part by horizon
thermodynamics, and **nothing in the corpus carried that before `r6437`.** ⌗ *And the entropy side is no
longer merely open: the no-free-constants claim has a stake in the branch, so it is a **prediction**
rather than an opening.*

**⌗ ② WAS DRAWN AS TWO ROOMS UNTIL `r6479`.** *`PO-30` wants a **law** --- why a cut bends as it does --- and `PO-41`'s strike says what is owed at the head is **the matter on its collapse leg, and how many**. ***Every lap after the first inherits its cut's shape from its progenitor; the head, by construction, inherits nothing*** --- so what fixes the head's cut is exactly a law that picks a cut absent a progenitor. ⇒ **`PO-30` discharged would discharge what the head owes; the converse fails, a fact about one cut yielding no law.** So the opening has a **direction**, and `PO-31`'s is the shadow of `PO-30`'s. ⌗ *The corpus draws this nowhere --- "generative law" never occurs within reach of "head", "genealogy" or "progenitor" in the register or any paper, the two rows having sat in different sectors since they were opened.* ⚠ *Held at weight: a **structural reading**, not a computation. **It fails if `PO-30`'s law fixes only the cut-content RELATION and not which cut**, and that is where to attack it.* \rcpt{P08_the_content_law_and_the_head_are_one_wall_seen_twice}

**⌗ ②'s HEAD HALF IS MOSTLY CLOSED ALREADY, and by rows that are struck.** *$\eta$, $A_s$ and $n_s$ all terminate at
the head --- `PO-41` for the first, `r6427` for the others once the stationary-recursion route was
excluded. ***One question asked of three quantities***, and the corpus already names what is owed there:
the matter on the head's collapse leg.*

**⌗ ③ HAS THE MOST OPENINGS AND ONE INSTRUMENT.** *`PO-24`'s residual is weighed on `PO-13`'s two-arm
machinery; `PO-25` hands *what stands in the horizon's place* to `PO-13`'s dynamical interior. **Two of the
three openings are runs rather than readings**, which is why this room moves slowest from a chat.*

**⛔ AND ④ IS NOT A ROOM.** *`PO-36` shares a wall with nothing. `r6407` established that both frameworks
take the same $m(r)$ --- whatever gravitates is the bend --- so the measurement **discriminates the dark
fraction and not this construction**, and it discharges by an observation nobody has made rather than by
corpus work. ⌗ ***It is carried here because it is honestly open, not because it bounds anything***, and a
later pass may find it belongs off this board.*

⚠ **THIS IS A JUDGEMENT, RECORDED SO ITS CHANGE IS VISIBLE, and it is not generated.** *An earlier sketch
of it was made from memory and was wrong within two turns of being said --- three of its rows moved. **Read
it against the rows, not instead of them.***

## ⛭ THE COMPLETENESS TEST — *`r3969`, and it found a gap, so it is worth keeping*

***The ledger and this register are two different populations, and neither is a superset of the other.***
*`corpus/open_ledger.txt` holds every qualification SENTENCE in the papers, all 96 now read.
`THE_OPEN_PROBLEMS_LEDGER`'s FAMILIES are a coarser grain over the same physics. **A row can be homed
correctly and a family still have no row**, which is exactly what happened.*

| family | live row |
|---|---|
| 1 · matter branch-point crossing | ⛭ **`PO-30`** — *the crossing is well posed; what remains is the generative law* |
| ~~2~~ | *killed `r1280`* |
| 3 · inherited datum, $\eta$ and the onset | ⛭ **`PO-31`** — ***opened `r3969` by this test; `PO-16`'s own text had named it since `r3012` and no row carried it*** |
| 4 · irreducible interior reassignments | **`PO-25`** — *charged half; Kerr half closed `r2378`* |
| 5 · scalar-perturbation sector | **`PO-13`** + **`PO-24`** |
| 6 · propagating fermion sector | **`PO-26`** |
| 7 · world-correspondence | **`PO-26`** *(`PO-27` struck `r4245`; its open half folded here)* |
| 8 · interacting quantum tower | **`PO-23`** |

⌗ ***Three live rows are finer than the family grain and belong to no family***: *`PO-29` (the $-M/r^{3}$
attribution), `PO-32` (horizon thermodynamics with no realised horizon, reopened `r4317`), `PO-36` (which mass
the Hubble--Eddington radius tracks), together with `PO-30`'s generative-law half. That is not a defect — it
is the register resolving what the families bundle.*

⇒ ***So the test to run before calling this complete is not "is every row homed" but "does every family
have a row, and does every row have a family or a stated reason it is finer."***

A finite computation of the same kind as `PO-23`'s, on a different quantity, and nobody knows whether the answer is zero.* &#9670; ***A NEGATIVE REPORTED BECAUSE IT IS THE FIRST THING A READER WILL REACH FOR:*** *no collision with the ghost-free result. A Weyl-squared term carries a massive spin-2 ghost, but `P07` scopes its claim in the same sentence that states it — the **classical** nonlinear regime is ghost-free to all orders — and a quantum counterterm does not reach a classical statement. **The claim needs no scoping it does not already have.*** &#9670; ***AND A SECOND POSSIBLE ENTRY, CHIRALITY-TIED, NOT WORKED:*** *`P10` notes the Pontryagin density is non-zero at second order for a circularly polarised mode and reverses with handedness, a linearly polarised mode returning zero — **and that the zero is a property of the mode chosen and not of the geometry**. Whether a parity-odd entry is owed therefore depends on the tower's polarisation, on which the corpus has a position in the matter sector. **Nobody has joined those two.*** &#9670; ***THE PARITY-ODD ENTRY IS NOT OWED ON PRESENT GROUNDS, `r4545` — AND THE TEMPTING CHAIN FAILS AT TWO SEPARATE JOINS.*** *The five faces give a $\mathbb{Z}_2$ exchanging the helicities and grading fermion chirality. **A map that exchanges two things is a grading, and every parity-even theory has one.** The corpus's own criterion is not that a reflection exists but that **no connected action completes it** — stated at `sec:derived` and applied to the radiating sector, never to this tower.* &#9670; ***AND IT FAILS INDEPENDENTLY AT THE COUNTERTERM ITSELF.*** *A counterterm answers to a parity-odd divergence of the effective action, a property of the theory and the state, **not of a basis mode**; the two helicities span the same space in either basis and cancel on a parity-symmetric state. **So `P10`'s caveat cuts against the chain rather than for it** — a mode-dependent quantity cannot fix the counterterm in either direction, which bounds the caveat's own instrument rather than deferring the question to a polarisation.* &#9670; ***AND EVEN IF OWED IT IS NOT THE SAME KIND OF THING AS THIS ROW'S ENTRY.*** *The density is a total derivative and contributes no field equation, so whether it is a ledger entry at all is a question about **whether the audit counts topological terms** — which has never arisen because this is the first candidate. The Weyl-squared entry is a genuine coupling with an uncosted coefficient. **Two potential entries, different in kind, and only one is a constant in the sense the audit means.*** &#9670; ***QUESTION (i) IS ANSWERED, `r4547`, AND THE ANSWER IS NO.*** *The closed layer is a group manifold, so its connected isometry group is a quotient of $SU(2)\times SU(2)$; the transverse-traceless harmonics at level $k$ sit in $(\tfrac{k+1}{2},\tfrac{k-3}{2})$ and its swap, **whose dimensions reproduce the tower's own degeneracy exactly at every level from the floor upward** — checked here, ten at the floor as a self-dual and an anti-self-dual five. **The two labels differ by two and never coincide.** A connected group acts within an isotypic component and cannot carry a representation to an inequivalent one, so the exchange is the outer automorphism and lies on the disconnected component. **The criterion is met in the strongest available form: the handedness is an irrep label.*** &#9670; ***SO THE TOWER IS CHIRALLY CAPABLE — WHICH IS THE RESULT, AND IT IS NOT THE ACHIRALITY BRANCH.*** *It is a second place where geometric chirality can live, and the sharper of the two: in the radiating sector the handedness is the sign of a conserved quantity, here it is a representation label. **And the join to the wall is sharper than both being chiral** — the index obstruction renders a connected-gauge fermion spectrum vector-like, and the tower is chiral in exactly the $SU(2)_L\times SU(2)_R$ sense that obstruction works in. Same group, same decomposition, opposite outcomes, one stated reason.* &#9670; ***AND THE PARITY-ODD ENTRY IS STILL NOT OWED — but the first of the two failures is removed, so the second now carries the whole verdict alone.*** *A counterterm answers to the effective action, and the effective action answers to **the state** rather than to the tower's capacity. **The marked step, and `63` marks it as one rather than assuming it:** the boundary condition is Hartle–Hawking regularity imposed fibre by fibre, and **nothing in its statement refers to helicity** — so the two towers are populated alike and the parity-odd content cancels between them. `63` has not run that check.* &#9670; ***AND IT REACHES THE LEDGER CLAIM NOT AT ALL.*** *The scoping stands as landed at eight sites; the Weyl-squared entry is parity-even and stands uncosted; the computed constant stands. **No rescue is offered and none is available here.*** &#9670; ***SO TWO QUESTIONS REPLACE IT, INDEPENDENT OF EACH OTHER AND NEITHER WORKED:*** *(i) does a connected isometry of the closed layer identify the two helicities of an $S^3$ tensor harmonic — a finite representation-theoretic question on the corpus's own criterion; and (ii) **does the ledger count topological terms**, which is a scope question about the audit rather than a physics question about the tower.* &#9670; ***NOT CLAIMED:*** *that the tower is achiral. What is claimed is that the argument for its being chiral needs a step the corpus does not have — and that the corpus asserts gravitational chirality for the radiating sector on a criterion it has not applied here, which is a different thing and not evidence for the first.* &#9670; ***QUESTION (ii) IS ANSWERED r6849 (66), FROM `P17`'s OWN TEXT, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT THE AUDIT DOES NOT RANGE OVER IT AND SHOULD NOT.*** *§ledger read whole partitions the constants two ways and says what each is: the real-geometric gauges $c,\Lambda,G$, each **a nameable feature of the substrate and its cuts**; and the thermal $\hbar,k_B$, standard horizon thermodynamics whose CR-specific content is **the closing of the one quantum freedom**, named there as the self-adjoint extension. **A topological term's finite part is neither**: it names no feature of the substrate, since a topological term contributes no field equation, and it is not the extension.* &#9670; ***AND THE AUDIT'S SUBJECT IS WHAT MAKES THIS SCOPING CORRECT RATHER THAN CONVENIENT.*** *The ledger counts what the construction must supply **to fix the geometry and close its one quantum freedom**. A coefficient whose only observable is a constant entropy shift enters no geometric prediction and no extension, so counting it would be counting a different kind of thing under the same name --- **which is the defect `r6437` made and `r6471` retracted, in the other direction.*** &#9670; ***SO THE ROW'S REMAINING CONTENT IS ONE UNRUN CHECK AND ONE DECLINED QUESTION, AND NEITHER IS THE ROW'S TITLE.*** *The check: whether Hartle--Hawking regularity, imposed fibre by fibre, populates the two helicity towers alike --- **nothing in its statement refers to helicity**, and if that holds the parity-odd content cancels between them and the entry is not owed at all. The declined question is whether $S=A/4$ carries to a cosmological horizon, which `P17` declines in terms and says would be a result rather than a gap were it to fail. **The row cannot close above the check**, and the check is routed.* &#9670; ***A CANDIDATE NAMED AND DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT:*** *a log needs a subtraction point, the corpus holds there is no second physical length, and in a discrete mode sum the subtraction point is a mode number — of which this tower has a canonical one, its own floor. **Whether that fixes the constant or merely names a convention is a question, not a result**, and if it is checked the burden is to show the floor is forced rather than convenient.* |
**⛭⛭⛭ STANDING ORDER r7013 (66, from measurement, and it COMPLETES r6861 rather than replacing it): A ROW'S REMAINDER EARNS A ROW ONLY IF IT IS A DIFFERENT KIND OF OBJECT --- AND EVERY ROW CARRIES TWO EXITS.** *`r6861` stops a remainder being buried, and that is right and it stands. **What it does not say is which remainders are rows**, and applied without that clause it manufactures a successor forever, because every instrument has a residual uncertainty and the residual uncertainty of an instrument is another instrument question.* &#9670; ⌗ ***THE MEASUREMENT, BECAUSE THIS IS NOT AN OPINION ABOUT PACE.*** *Every remainder chain in this register's history is **three rows deep or shorter** --- except one, which reached **four** inside eighteen revisions of a single day: `PO-65` → `PO-67` → `PO-68` → `PO-69`. And **7 of 58 rows carry a second exit**, a stated way to finish WITHOUT delivering the object: `PO-15`, `PO-19`, `PO-21`, `PO-23`, `PO-31`, `PO-47`, `PO-56` --- **every one a physics row, and not one of the nine-row reproducibility sequence among them**.* ⇒ *** SO THE CORRELATION HAS A MECHANISM: A ROW THAT CAN ONLY FINISH BY SUCCEEDING TURNS EVERY FAILURE-TO-DELIVER INTO A NARROWER ROW. *** ⌗ *`PO-47` is the case that shows the second exit is not a loophole --- **it was struck because its stopping rule fired**, the sky being unable to tell the two models apart there, and nobody delivered what it asked for.* &#9670; ⛭ ***SO A REMAINDER IS ONE OF THREE THINGS AND ONLY THE FIRST IS A ROW.*** **① A DIFFERENT KIND OF OBJECT** --- *a question the closed row exposed and did not ask.* **That is `r6861`'s case and it is a row**, with what is already ruled out carried across. **② THE SAME QUESTION AT FINER RESOLUTION** --- *the residual uncertainty of what was just built.* **Not a row**: it is a stated limit written at the site where it acts, *and that is a **finished outcome** and not a deferral.* **③ A REMAINDER WHOSE DISCHARGE IS ALREADY KNOWN** --- *a named change nobody has made.* **Not a row: an ORDER.** *A row is for something whose discharge is not yet known, and a queue entry wearing a row's clothes is how a four-deep chain gets to five.* &#9670; ⛭⛭ ***AND EVERY ROW CARRIES TWO EXITS, WRITTEN WHEN IT IS OPENED.*** *A row finishes when its object is **delivered**, **or** when it is demonstrated that no argument available to this construction supplies that object --- and **the second is a RESULT, which the papers carry**. Without it a row cannot finish except by succeeding, which is the generator above.* ⇒ **`check_remainder_chains` enforces both halves**: *chain depth against the register's own historical ceiling, and a discharge clause and a terminal clause on every open row.* ⌗ ***And none of this narrows what narrowing is for.*** *Narrowing IS the work, and this order forbids only narrowing with no floor under it --- the floor being that a row must be able to end without winning.* &#9670; ⭭ ***AMENDED r7027 --- AND THE AMENDMENT IS THE RULE'S OWN DEFECT ARRIVING IN THE FILE IT GOVERNS.*** *`r7013` required both exits on every open row and `check_remainder_chains` enforced their **presence**. **This file is append-forward, so an amended exit APPENDS and never supersedes** --- and a reader coming down a row meets the OLDEST clause first with nothing to tell it from the current one.* ⇒ *** MEASURED AT `r7027`: `PO-56` CARRIED **FIVE** TERMINAL CLAUSES AND **EIGHT** DISCHARGE CLAUSES, SET ACROSS FIVE REVISIONS OF ONE DAY --- EACH RIGHT WHEN IT WAS MADE, ONLY THE LAST LIVE, AND THE GATE REPORTED THE ROW GREEN THROUGHOUT. *** ⌗ ***Which is this corpus's most frequent defect landing inside the gate built to stop the previous one***: *a check run on one property --- is a clause present --- licensing a conclusion about another --- which clause is current.* ⇒ **So every open row now carries exactly one `⛭ THE LIVE CLAUSE` block naming the revision that set it, the gate requires exactly one, and the clause counts above it are printed rather than hidden.** *The history stays, because the record of how an exit moved is worth keeping; what changes is that a reader is told where the row actually stands.*

**⛭⛭ STANDING ORDER r6861 (Daryl): A ROW'S REMAINDER EARNS A ROW.** *When a row is struck, whatever it leaves live --- an edge it moved, a question it closed above, a candidate it named and did not build --- **is opened as its own row in the same pass**, with what is already ruled out carried across so the new row starts where the old one ended. A remainder recorded only in the struck row's text, in a paper's prose or in the changelog is buried: those are records, and **a node works from the frontier**. The five rows below were opened on this order after nine rows became two when they should have been seven --- a smaller seven, with a more consolidated corpus propping them up to be attacked the same way.*

the fix is one clause and it is the clause the passage's ‘with nothing fitted’ rests on. ⑪ `P12`'s abstract and header stop at $\mathrm{Aut}(A_2)$ order twelve and say nothing of the order-48 closure, which is the paper's strongest discrete result. ⑫ `P17` l.412 defines $\alpha\equiv\sqrt{3/|\Lambda|}$ where the section turns on $\alpha^{2}=3/\Lambda$ with $\alpha^{2}<0$ admitted — one of the two should drop the modulus. ⑬ `P18` l.1392 forbids the rate assignment §`tensions` requires, naming the sound horizon where the corpus's own statement of that fatal extension names **the distances**. ⑭ `THE_PLAN` carries `P16`'s superseded order count and an alias hygiene item for an alias that no longer exists. ⑮ Six uncited bibitems across five papers — an editorial call about whether each reference belongs, not a defect. ⑯ Two further instruments the read names and this pass did not build: **declared counts against enumerated items**, which `THE_PLAN` Phase 6 already names, and **a superseded-numeric-value ledger** grepped corpus-wide, which is the channel that produced the retired-driving figure and the index's four stale numbers.* &#9670; ⛭ ***THREE WORKED r6901 (66) --- ①, ⑤ AND ⑥, THE THREE WITH PHYSICS IN THEM.*** *① **The identity is conceded and the convergence is relocated onto what carries it.** On the marginal congruence $\dd^{2}r/\dd\tilde\tau^{2}=-f'/2=r\,K_G$ holds by differentiating $f$, in one line --- so the slice being flat and the areal acceleration changing sign are **one fact in two languages** and are counted once. ⇒ *What is not an identity is what the section is about*: $r^{3}=M\alpha^{2}$ is **one formula read at two masses**, a local mass giving the boundary within which a structure stays bound and the cosmological offset --- which on this construction **is** the epoch parameter --- giving the acceleration turn, and they are one formula because the two masses are the same parameter of the same metric. *Computed from different objects, as a local exterior and a Friedmann background are, they would share $\Lambda$ and nothing else.* **That is stronger than the coincidence it replaces, and it is the causal reassignment doing the work.** &#9670; The corpus's own statement names **the distances**, and `P18` now says so: carrying the radiation-included law into the distances pins them to a radiation-era density the foliation's rate does not carry, **and it is the distances and not the plasma's own lengths**, which take that rate by the same rule that puts nucleosynthesis there. ⇒ *So the two windows fall on the same side of the assignment and only the separations read across leaves fall on the other* --- which is what makes the section's own ‘both sectors on exactly one reading’ true as stated.* &#9670; ⌗ ***EIGHT REMAIN.*** &#9670; ⛭ ***FOUR MORE WORKED r6905 (66) --- ⑦, ⑧, ⑨ AND ⑩.*** *⑦ **The exclusion needed two arguments, not one.** $\mathfrak{su}(2,1)$ does fall to the count: it preserves a Hermitian form of signature $(2,1)$, so its realified carrier is six-dimensional bearing a real form of signature $(4,2)$, which lands it in $\mathfrak{so}(4,2)$. **But $\mathfrak{sl}(3,\mathbb{R})$ acts faithfully on $\mathbb{R}^{3}$, so the count does not reach it** --- it falls instead to real rank: the substrate's $\mathfrak{so}(5,1)$ has real rank one, $\mathfrak{sl}(3,\mathbb{R})$ has real rank two, and a subalgebra's rank cannot exceed its ambient's. ⇒ *The wall holds for all three forms; what was wrong was presenting one argument as doing work it cannot do.* &#9670; ***⑧ AND THE PARENTHETICAL WAS OFF ON BOTH COUNTS, CHECKED HERE.*** *$\su(3)$ preserves $h=g+i\omega$ on $\mathbb{C}^{3}$, and $\mathfrak{so}(6)$ is the stabiliser of the **symmetric** part $g$ --- the antisymmetric $\omega$ is what gives $\mathfrak{sp}$ instead. And $\mathbf{3}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{3}}$ is the carrier's **complexification**, not the carrier. ⛔ ***ITEM TWO, AND IT IS A CONFLATION IN AN INSTRUMENT THAT HAS OTHERWISE BEEN THE MOST CAREFUL IN THE CORPUS.*** *The tolerance sweep failed on a branch head with **zero flagged sites**: the non-zero exit was its incompleteness notice, one receipt not finishing on one build against an 1800-second budget. **Measured by the seat that hit it, that receipt runs in under a minute plain, under two at one thread, under a minute at four** --- a factor of about sixteen from any measured cost, not thread-dependent, on a build that itself took nearly twice as long as its siblings.* ⇒ *** SO A LONGER BUDGET WOULD RECORD A COST THAT DOES NOT EXIST, AGAINST THAT DICTIONARY'S OWN CONVENTION --- THE FIX BELONGS TO THE SWEEP AND NOT TO THE RECEIPT. *** ⌗ *And the conflation is the real defect: **"nothing flagged, one receipt unmeasured" and "a site flagged" are different findings and the exit code says the same thing about both**, so a reader who trusts the exit code learns the wrong one.* &#9670; **WHAT WOULD DISCHARGE IT:** *exit conditions that separate a flagged site from an unmeasured receipt, so that "not a sweep" and "a sweep that found something" are distinguishable without reading the log; a timed-out probe retried once serially before it is called unmeasured; and **a statement of what the carry can and cannot say about a timeout**, since it will keep carrying them. ⛔ *And not by lengthening a budget to cover a cost nobody has measured, which is the defect one field over.* &#9670; ⭭⭭ ***`r7009` (`r7007+70.1`) --- ALL THREE DISCHARGE ITEMS DELIVERED, AND ITEM TWO'S DEFECT WAS WORSE THAN THIS ROW SAID.*** *The row said the two findings **shared** an exit code. They did not merely share one: **the instrument HID the flag.** `sweep_tolerances --compare` returned `2` for "not a sweep" **before it looked at the flags**, so a run that both flagged a site and failed to measure a receipt reported only "not a sweep"; `sweep_runner_reads --report` had the mirror image, returning `1` on a flag **before** looking for receipts it never traced; and the CI step collapsed whatever came back to `rc=1`.* ⇒ ***So a reader of either exit code could learn the wrong finding in BOTH directions.*** ✔ ***Now `1` = flagged, `2` = not a sweep, `3` = both, in both tools, each printing a closing `VERDICT:` line naming which; the CI step ORs the two comparisons' codes bit by bit instead of collapsing them; and the unmeasured list is no longer cut off at twelve.*** ⌗ ***Seeded both ways, and the seeding is the whole of it:*** *all four cases give their own code in both tools, and **the old code run on the "both" case returns `2`** --- the defect shown and not only described.* &#9670; ✔ ***ITEM TWO: A TIMED-OUT PROBE IS RE-RUN ONCE, ALONE, AT THE SAME BUDGET, BEFORE IT IS FILED UNMEASURED.*** *Not a lengthened budget --- **that would record a cost the receipt does not have**, which is the defect one field over and was forbidden in the order. **Both attempts are kept**, the first as `first_attempt`, so a receipt that finishes only when run alone stays visible as exactly that, and a second timeout stays a timeout and is still unmeasured. **Every probe log now records `wall` and `budget`**, which is what `L274/H1`'s "nearly twice as long on one build" had to be reconstructed by hand for.* ⌗ ***And the same retry went into `sweep_runner_reads`, on a correction the seat made against itself:*** *its first draft of the reply said that tool traced one receipt at a time; it checked before opening the PR, found the trace also runs a pool at `--jobs 4`, **and put the retry in rather than the sentence**.* ⌗ *Not retried, and stated: the suite runner's `[slow]` cap, which is the heavy and scoped jobs' own cap on a plain run and not a probe --- retrying there would change what "over timeout" means for the suite's verdict, which this order did not license.* &#9670; ⭭ ***ITEM THREE: WHAT THE CARRY CAN AND CANNOT CLAIM ABOUT A TIMEOUT, WRITTEN INTO `red_carry.py` AT THE SITE THAT CARRIES ONE.*** *It **can** claim that a timeout is carried like any other red, so no push that misses it silences it. It **cannot** claim that a timeout's clear is a repair: every other clear is a run that covered the receipt and passed on it, where a timeout's next green shows only that it finished once, on that runner, at that load --- **and a quieter machine clears it exactly as a fix would**. And it **cannot place a timeout's birth**, a timeout not being tree state, which is why `PO-65`'s 68 per cent excluded them by name.* &#9670; ⇒⇒ ***THE ROW CLOSES ON ALL THREE ITEMS --- SEPARATED, RETRIED, STATED --- AND ITS REMAINDER IS `PO-68`:*** *the layer itself names the one place a flickering receipt is visible, the carry ledger's own history, **and nothing reads it** --- so a receipt carried, cleared and carried again is a pattern the layer can see and does not report.* | ~~**STRUCK r7009** — opened r7007, all three discharge items delivered r7009.** *Prior state: carried inside `PO-65` as the exclusion its own measurement named, and inside a routed note from the acoustic seat; neither could be a row until that row closed around them.* | `scripts/sweep_tolerances.py`; `scripts/red_carry.py`; the `LONG` budget dictionary |
| ~~**PO-65**~~ | **A SCOPED JOB'S VERDICT IS A STATEMENT ABOUT THE PUSH AND NOT ABOUT THE TREE, SO A RED IS SILENCED BY THE NEXT PUSH THAT HAPPENS TO MISS IT.** &#9670; ⛭⛭ ***OPENED r6993 (66) ON A FINDING BY THE ACOUSTIC SEAT, WHICH DOES NOT OWN THIS LAYER AND WAS NOT LOOKING FOR IT --- AND THAT IS PART OF WHY IT IS BELIEVABLE.*** *`PO-62` wired three jobs that scope each push to the receipts that push can affect, which is what made per-push detection affordable at all and is why that row was struck. **What nobody checked is what a scoped red MEANS once the next push lands.** A red scoped job records a verdict about the commits that produced it; the next push computes a new scope from its own commits, and if that scope does not cover the red receipt the job goes **green with nothing repaired** --- and nothing anywhere records that the earlier red was never answered.* &#9670; ⌷ ***AND IT IS OBSERVED TWICE, ONCE DELIBERATELY AND ONCE IN THE HISTORY:*** *both of one branch's scoped reds went green on a one-commit push with **nothing repaired**; and the same mechanism explains why `main`'s tolerance job went green after `r6981` **while three flagged sites in a `P10` receipt sat exactly where they were** --- sites a later revision then found and repaired by reading them rather than by being told. ⇒ ***SO THE ROW IS NOT A HYPOTHETICAL: THE GAP HAS ALREADY SWALLOWED A REAL RED ON `main`.*** &#9670; ⛔⛭ ***AND WHAT IT IS, NAMED PRECISELY, IS THE STRUCK ROW'S OWN CLASS ONE LEVEL UP.*** *`PO-60` was about greens that certify nothing --- a bare literal, a read that never happens, a tolerance the machine sets. **This is a green that certifies nothing because the question changed underneath it**: the detector ran correctly, on a scope computed correctly, and the resulting green is still not a statement that the tree is clean.* ⌗ *Which is why it belongs in this family and not in `PO-62`, whose own claim --- that scoping loses no recall **at the push that makes a defect** --- is untouched and remains measured at ten of ten.* &#9670; ⛭⛭ ***AND THE CALL IS MADE AT THE OPENING RATHER THAN CARRIED AS A CHOICE, BECAUSE THE TWO CANDIDATES ARE NOT EQUALLY GOOD.*** *The two shapes on offer were to **carry the last red scope forward** until a push actually covers it, or to **let the monthly backstop close** the reds. ⇒ *** IT IS CARRY-FORWARD. **WHAT WOULD DISCHARGE IT:** *a red scope that persists in the repository rather than in a job's history, union-ed into every later push's scope until a run covers it and passes; the carry cleared only by a green on the receipts that were red, never by time and never by a push that missed them; ⛭⛭ ***AND r7001 (66, on node 70's `r6999+70.1`) TAKES THE LEDGER ITSELF, WHICH IS BUILT, SEEDED AND COSTED --- AND THE ONE THING IT CANNOT DO IS THE ONE THING NO NODE CAN DO.*** &#9670; ⌷ ***WHAT IS BUILT.*** *`scripts/red_carry.py`: every scoped job writes what it found for its class to a ledger on every push, and every scoped job runs its natural scope **union everything the ledger carries**. ⇒ *A carried receipt leaves the ledger **only** when a run that included it came back green on it --- never by time, never by a push that missed it (the union means no push misses it), and **never by a green on a different scope**, a green clearing only the receipts it ran.* ⌗ *And when the job failed but no receipt can be named --- the install died, the job hit its limit --- **the whole scope is carried: a red nobody can attribute is still a red**, which is the `PO-60` discipline reapplied one level up.* &#9670; ⛭ ***AND WHERE IT LIVES IS CHOSEN WITH THE THREE ALTERNATIVES REFUSED IN WRITING:*** *a ref outside the branch namespace, as a chain of commits each saying which push, which class and what moved. **Not a file on the branch** --- the job would commit to the branch it is testing, moving the head under the seat that pushed it, and a commit made with the workflow's own token triggers no run, so the new head would carry **no verdict at all**. **Not the job's history** --- artifacts and caches expire and are not what a seat reads. ⌗ *And a ref outside the branch namespace is not fetched by a plain fetch, so it cannot be mistaken for a branch by the gate that tests containment.* ⌷ *Concurrency is a fast-forward push that re-reads and re-applies its own delta when refused, and **two jobs disagreeing about one receipt resolve red.*** &#9670; ⛔⛔ ***AND IT IS NOT WIRED, FOR A REASON THAT IS NOT A NODE'S TO REMOVE: HOLDING THE LEDGER NEEDS THE WORKFLOW'S TOKEN TO HAVE WRITE ACCESS, AND THAT IS A REPOSITORY SETTING ON THE OWNER'S ACCOUNT.*** *The seat built the whole of what it can build and stopped exactly where its authority ends, rather than wiring something that would fail on first use or asking for the permission by widening the workflow and hoping.* ⌗ *This seat confirmed the limit rather than relaying it: **the Actions permissions endpoint is not reachable from the gating container either**, so which of the two halves is missing --- the repository default or the per-workflow grant --- cannot be read from here.* ⇒ *** SO THE ROW'S REMAINING WORK IS ONE SETTING AND THEN ONE WIRING, AND THE SETTING IS THE FIRST THING IN THIS PROGRAMME ALL DAY THAT A NODE CANNOT DO FOR ITSELF. *** ⌗ *Stated plainly rather than carried as a note, per the standing rule that a decision genuinely belonging upward is named in the first sentence that mentions it.*  &#9670; ⛭⛭ ***AND r7003 (66, on node 70's `r7001+70.2`) HAS IT WIRED, THE PERMISSION HAVING BEEN GRANTED --- AND THE GRANT IS NARROWED EXACTLY AS THE ORDER ASKED RATHER THAN TAKEN AT ITS WIDEST.*** *The repository's workflow token was set to read-write by its owner, and the seat then **narrowed it in the workflow**: the root is `contents: read`, and only the three scoped jobs carry `contents: write`, only for the record step, which pushes the one ref and nothing else.* ⇒ *** SO A PERMISSION GRANTED AT THE REPOSITORY'S WIDEST WAS SPENT AT THE NARROWEST THE JOB NEEDS, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT A GRANT USUALLY PRODUCES. *** ⌗ *Every scoped job now runs its natural scope **union what the ledger carries** and records what it found, so the union is the mechanism by which no push can miss a carried red.* &#9670; ⌷ ***AND THE ROW'S OWN MEASUREMENT IS IN FLIGHT RATHER THAN DONE:*** *the tool to make it is committed --- it re-runs each receipt known to have gone red at every push that could change its state, and **counts the pushes at which that red was silent** --- but the number is not in yet. ⇒ *So this row is wired but not measured, and **the thing it is waiting on is the evidence for its own urgency** rather than for its remedy.* ⌗ *And the order that asked for it said in terms that a low number would be reported as a low number: the remedy would still be right and its urgency lower.*  &#9670; ⛭⛭⛭⛭ ***AND r7007 (66, on node 70's `r7003+70.1`) STRIKES IT: THE SILENCING WAS THE RULE AND NOT THE EXCEPTION --- **358 OF 528 RED RECEIPT-PUSHES, 68 PER CENT, WERE SILENT** --- AND THE CARRY HAS ALREADY RUN A WHOLE CYCLE ON ANOTHER SEAT'S BRANCH WITHOUT BEING TOLD ABOUT IT.*** &#9670; ⌷ ***HOW IT WAS MEASURED, AND THE ASSUMPTION IT RESTS ON WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED:*** *each receipt known to have gone red was run at **every** push of the last four hundred that could change it, its class's own scope; between two such pushes nothing it reads moves, so its state is constant. **And that constancy is the read index's claim, so every receipt was also run at four pushes outside its scope --- 0 of 48 disagreed with the stretch they sat in.*** &#9670; ⛭⛭ ***AND THE NUMBERS ARE WORSE THAN THE ROW ARGUED FROM TWO OBSERVATIONS.*** *Twelve receipts, **every one of them silent on most of its red pushes**, the lowest at 49 per cent. **The tolerance class is the sharpest at 67 to 97 per cent**, its scopes being small --- two to thirty-two pushes out of four hundred --- *so a red there is asked about again almost never*.* ⇒ **And carrying all of it would have cost 4,683 seconds over four hundred pushes, about twelve seconds a push**, most of it one receipt. ⌗ *The order said a low number would be reported as low. **It was not low, and the number is a LOWER BOUND**: it counts only receipts known to have gone red, and timeouts are excluded because they are not tree state and a replay that re-runs a tree cannot place them.* &#9670; ⛭⛭⛭ ***AND THE LIVE DEMONSTRATION IS BETTER THAN THE REPLAY, BECAUSE NOBODY ARRANGED IT.*** *The carry ref holds four commits, **all from another seat's branch**: a push went red and the suite and tolerance jobs each carried one; ⌷ ***AND ONE LIMIT WAS MET IN PRACTICE RATHER THAN ONLY DOCUMENTED:*** *the first pass over one receipt used a stride of eight and **the stride missed its red**, which rose and was answered inside one stride. ⛔ ***SO WHAT IS LEFT OF THIS ROW IS ITEM TWO ALONE, AND IT IS UNTOUCHED:*** *the interpreter's patch in the fingerprint, and the two-patch probe that would say whether it belongs there. **No measurement has been run and the guess is still forbidden.***  &#9670; ⛭⛭⛭ ***AND r7007 (66, on node 70's `r7003+70.1`) STRIKES IT: THE INTERPRETER'S PATCH MOVES NOTHING, MEASURED, SO THE FINGERPRINT NOW READS THE MINOR VERSION --- AND THAT IS A GATE RELAXED **WITH** A MEASUREMENT, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THIS ROW FORBADE.*** &#9670; Both are receipts that PASS while asserting nothing about what they name, which is the one failure mode a ratchet on the red count cannot see.* &#9670; ⓵ ***THE VACUOUS GREEN: a check pinned to a bare numeric literal that matches the document somewhere other than the sentence the check is about.*** *Five were found inside the 83: `C16`'s `'1.082'`, and the `'8.2'` conjunct in `C24`, `C25`, `C28` and `L557` ⓹. **A literal short enough to recur is not a pin, it is a coincidence with a passing exit code** --- and the shorter the number the more of the corpus it matches, so the class grows with the corpus rather than shrinking.* &#9670; ⓶ ***THE NEVER-GREEN-UNDER-THE-RUNNER: a receipt that has never run in the environment the suite runs it in.*** *Four were found inside the 83 --- `P03` T, `P03` w, `P14` lifts, `P17` ledgers --- born reading paths relative to the repository root while the runner runs from the family directory. ⛔ ***And two of the four PASSED ON AN EMPTY GLOB***, *which is the sharp form: a check that iterates a glob and asserts over its members is vacuously true when the glob is empty, so a receipt can be green for years while reading nothing at all.* &#9670; ⌗ ***WHY THIS IS A ROW AND NOT A BATCH OF NINE REPAIRS.*** *All nine were found because something ELSE made them run --- a debt sweep with an unrelated cause. **Nobody has ever looked for either class across the whole 849**, and neither class announces itself: the first reports success, the second reports success faster. ⇒ *The nine are the sample, not the population, and a sample found by accident bounds nothing.** &#9670; ⛭⛭ ***AND A THIRD CLASS ARRIVED AT r6947, FOUND THE SAME WAY --- BY RUNNING, NOT BY SWEEPING --- WHICH IS THE ROW'S OWN POINT MADE TWICE.*** *Node 60's `r6946` receipt reported `rc=0` on the seat that wrote it and went **red on the gating tree**: a check comparing a closed form against direct diagonalization read $2.5\times10^{-9}$ there and $5.1\times10^{-7}$ here, against a threshold of $10^{-7}$.* ⇒ ***THE TOLERANCE HAD BEEN SET AT THE AUTHORING MACHINE'S ROUND-OFF FLOOR.*** *The second difference $(E(+\lambda)+E(-\lambda))/2-E(0)$ cancels to order $\lambda^{2}$, so the quotient carries $\varepsilon/\lambda^{2}$ --- **noise that GROWS as the step falls**, at a size that depends on the BLAS and the eigensolver build. Measured across three truncations the error is **non-monotone in $\lambda$** with its balance point at $3\times10^{-3}$, which is the signature saying the shipped step sat below the balance and the check was measuring round-off rather than agreement.* ⌗ *Repaired by scanning the step and asserting the minimum --- the best achievable agreement rather than the agreement at an arbitrary point, which is stronger and portable, with four decades of margin on a claim whose failure mode is $O(1)$.* ⇒⇒ **THE CLASS, STATED AS A CLASS: a tolerance calibrated from one run on one machine certifies that machine.** *It is the vacuous green's sibling --- the check passes for a reason that has nothing to do with what it is about --- and it is invisible to the red count for as long as nobody else runs it. ⚠ **And it is NOT detectable by reading**: only a second machine, or a deliberate perturbation of the numerical parameters, separates a converged tolerance from a floor. So the sweep needs a third detector and it cannot be a static one.* &#9670; ⛭⛭⛭ ***AND `r6931+70.3` SWEEPS BOTH OF THE FIRST TWO CLASSES WHOLE, WITH EACH DETECTOR SEEDED BOTH WAYS AND PRECISION ESTABLISHED BEFORE RECALL.*** *Two tools under `scripts/`, neither wired into CI, each stating its own recall limits in its head.* &#9670; ⓐ ***THE VACUOUS GREEN --- STRUCTURAL, A FEW SECONDS.*** *It takes every presence test whose needle is a bare number and whose haystack is a live paper or root register, locates every digit-bounded match, and flags the pin when no match sits within $400$ characters of the check's own context. **Not flagged: four or more significant digits at a single site, where coincidence is not credible; and pins into a fixed commit, which cannot drift.*** ⇒ *** FOUR MORE INSTANCES WERE LIVE AT HEAD BEYOND THE FIVE `PO-59` FOUND --- `C22` ⓷, `C27` ⓷, `C36` ⓵, `C41` ⓶ --- all held up by the same two coincidences, plus `C28` ⓶ found by hand in the receipt-source bucket, green on another receipt's history comment after that receipt's value had moved. All five repaired, each read where the figure stood and paired with an assertion of the paper's current value; after repair the sweep flags $0$. *** ⌗ ***Precision measured by reading every site:*** *all $20$ live-document pins at head read by hand including the unflagged ones --- $15$ read their own sentence and the $5$ flagged were all true. **Zero false positives and zero misses on that population.** And on the pre-repair tree it flags all five known instances plus the four above, and nothing else.* ⚠ *Measured and excluded from judgement: of $6$ flags on receipt-SOURCE haystacks, $1$ was true and $5$ false, a receipt repeating its own figure in docstring, table and assert --- **so co-location is the wrong test there**.* &#9670; ⓑ ***THE NEVER-GREEN-UNDER-THE-RUNNER --- DYNAMIC, THE COST OF A SUITE RUN.*** *It runs every registered receipt exactly as the runner does and observes every open, glob and directory listing. **FLAGGED means a RELATIVE read that resolved to nothing, which is the class exactly and covers the sharp form: a relative glob empty while the receipt exits 0.*** ⇒ *** FLAGGED $0$ at head over all $858$, every receipt traced to the end; $2$ reached triage and were read and judged legitimate, both INDEX-token resolvers probing roots. *** ⌗ ***Seeded on a real population:*** *the pre-repair tree traced whole --- $855$ receipts, about $80$ of them red --- **FLAGGED exactly $4$, and they are exactly the four `PO-59` found**. ⚠ ***WHAT NEITHER TOOL CAN SEE, STATED BY THEM RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER:*** *ⓐ cannot see a needle built at run time, a regex pin, a haystack whose file is not named where it is assigned, or a distinctive number sitting alone in the wrong sentence --- **so it bounds the class from below, with no false positives, and not from above.** ⓑ cannot see a read made in a subprocess the receipt spawns, or one through a C extension.* ⌗ *Wiring ⓑ into CI is a suite run's cost and is this seat's call, deferred one revision: the two tools are new and their own seeding is one revision old.* &#9670; ⛔ ***AND THE THIRD CLASS IS NOT SWEPT, WHICH IS WHY THIS ROW DOES NOT CLOSE.*** *The machine-dependent tolerance `r6947` added has no detector. **It is the one of the three that is not detectable by reading**, and the two tools above are both static in the sense that matters --- ⓐ parses source and ⓑ observes file access, and neither perturbs a numerical parameter. ⌗ *Node 60's own answer to it is the shape to generalise: **of each tolerance, ask whether the arithmetic could have been exact instead** --- and where it genuinely cannot, report the float against an exact predicted value rather than against a threshold, as `r6954` did against $-3/4$.* &#9670; **WHAT WOULD DISCHARGE IT:** *the complete unfiltered count of each class across all registered receipts, measured rather than estimated, with each instance repaired or named; and for each class, a detector seeded BOTH WAYS --- a clean tree measures nothing, so the sweep has to catch a planted instance and let a legitimate one through.* ⚠ ***THE FALSE-POSITIVE SIDE IS THE HARD HALF AND IS NAMED IN ADVANCE:*** *a structural check with no external literal is legitimate --- 81 of the 437 are UNPINNED-only and correctly so for a symbolic identity --- and a glob that is empty because the thing it globs was legitimately removed is not a defect either. **A sweep that cannot tell those from the nine will convert a real finding into a pile of noise, which is how a class like this gets dismissed.***  &#9670; ⛭⛭⛭⛭ ***AND r6975 (66, on node 70's r6961+70.2) STRIKES IT: THE THIRD CLASS HAS A DETECTOR, AND IT IS THE ONE THE ROW SAID COULD NOT BE STATIC.*** *`scripts/sweep_tolerances.py`. **The perturbation is the BUILD rather than a parameter, and which build mattered was measured on the real instance rather than guessed**: run on every kernel at one thread and at four, `r6946`'s own receipt gives $3.4\times10^{-7}$ at one thread (red, as on this gating seat) and $2.5\times10^{-9}$ at four --- the authoring seat's exact number --- while the kernel alone at one thread moves it by nothing.* ⇒ *** THE RUNNER PINS ONE THREAD AND AN INTERACTIVE AUTHOR RUNS ON EVERY CORE: THAT IS HOW THE INSTANCE HAPPENED, AND IT IS A MECHANISM RATHER THAN A CORRELATION. *** &#9670; ⌷ ***THE POPULATION IS MEASURED AND NOT ESTIMATED, WHICH THE ROW ASKED FOR.*** *The static pass finds $8{,}220$ numeric comparisons in $796$ receipts; **source cannot tell a float from an integer, so every registered receipt was run with every comparison instrumented** --- all $868$ passing instrumented, so the instrumentation changes nothing --- and **$3{,}237$ actually compare floats**, against $4{,}079$ exact or symbolic, $681$ non-numeric and $220$ on branches that do not execute. ⌗ *The $60$ float `==` were read in sample: deliberate exact-zero and bit-identity assertions, config reads and sign comparisons, none of which changes verdict between builds.* &#9670; ⛭ ***SEEDED BOTH WAYS, WHICH IS THE HALF THE ROW NAMED AS HARD.*** *Flagged: a second difference at a step far below its balance. **Let through: a converged eigenvalue with $10^{5}$ of headroom, and a truncation-dominated finite difference with thin headroom --- because a genuinely approximate claim is entitled to that.*** ⇒ *And on the real instance the one-thread-against-four-thread comparison is a pass/fail **flip**, which is exactly what the detector exists to catch and what the kernel-only comparison would have missed.* &#9670; ⌷ ***PRECISION BY READING EVERY SITE: 5 TRUE OF 7 FLAGGED, 3 TRUE OF 4 RECEIPTS, AND BOTH FALSE POSITIVES ARE ONE DESIGN.*** *A threshold scaled by a floor measured in the same run recalibrates itself --- a larger floor makes the check stricter --- and structurally the detector cannot tell that from a floor-reader. **Named as the residual false-positive class rather than tuned away.*** ⌗ *And $7$ further sites sit at the precision floor, $2\times10^{-15}$ to $4\times10^{-14}$ against $10^{-12}$; each was read, each is an identity in floating point with bounded cancellation, and they are counted rather than flagged.* &#9670; ⛭⛭ ***THE TWO TRUE INSTANCES 70 REPAIRED, FROM MEASUREMENT:*** *`no_state`'s floor was ONE eigenvector's variance, $1.4\times10^{-14}$ to $4.3\times10^{-13}$ across kernels against a constant $5.7\times10^{-14}$ --- **a flip**; And the freeze-out toy's fourth digit was **the solver's step sequence**: scanning both legs' tolerances over sixteen runs puts the endpoint anywhere in $0.9951$--$0.9987$ while the relic abundance agrees to $10^{-14}$, so it now pins what the computation determines.* &#9670; ⛔⛭ ***AND THE THREE SITES 70 NAMED RATHER THAN REPAIRED ARE THIS SEAT'S OWN, AND THE MEASUREMENT CONTRADICTS THIS SEAT'S ARGUMENT RATHER THAN EXTENDING IT.*** *`r6947` repaired that receipt by scanning the step and asserting the minimum --- the right instrument --- and then wrote that $10^{-6}$ was "four decades of margin while being above every machine's floor". **Measured across three builds the same site reads $2.5\times10^{-9}$, $7.3\times10^{-8}$ and $1.5\times10^{-7}$, so the margin was $6.7$ to $13.6$ times on two builds of three**, and the other two sites $4.2$--$5.3\times$ and $2.4$--$3.7\times$.* ⇒ *** THE SCAN FIXED WHERE THE STEP SITS AND DID NOT FIX THE MARGIN, AND THE MARGIN IS THE PART A SECOND MACHINE SEES. *** ⌷ *Repaired at `r6975` an order of magnitude above the worst measured value at each site --- every failure mode there is $O(1)$, so the widened tolerances keep about five decades of real margin and stop certifying one machine --- and verified green on two builds. **70's stronger route, carrying the second difference in exact arithmetic, is named and declined with its reason: the quantity is an eigenvalue of a truncated matrix, so exact arithmetic would change the instrument rather than its tolerance.*** &#9670; ⌷ ***AND ONE ITEM OUTSIDE THE CLASS, FIXED BECAUSE IT IS A HAZARD WHATEVER IT DECIDES:*** *a rank helper tested an SVD coefficient with `c == 0.0`, an EXACT zero against a value carrying round-off, and the guard fired a different number of times on two builds. | ~~**PO-58**~~ | **THE LIFT'S NORMALIZABILITY THRESHOLD, ON THE OPERATOR THE CORPUS NOW USES.** &#9670; ⛭ ***OPENED r6921 (66) FROM THE SPIN-UP 69 READ, AS THE REMAINDER OF A FIX THAT LANDED ONLY HALF.*** *`P14`'s count passage rejected the growing zero-mode branch by a leaf-measure argument at the branch point: $\dd\ell\sim\sqrt{\lvert r\rvert/2M}\,\dd r$, so $\lvert r\rvert^{s}$ normalizes for $s>-\tfrac34$ and the growing $s=-\lambda$ would need $\lambda<\tfrac34$, which no $\lambda=j+\tfrac12$ attains. **On the operator the corpus now uses that argument is gone**: the read corrected the count passage to the current form, where near the branch point $f\to-2M/r$ makes the exponent $\propto i\sqrt{\lvert r\rvert}$, both chiralities a bounded phase and the measure integrable, so ***the crossing selects nothing and costs the norm nothing***\rcpt{W2_the_wall_zero_mode_on_the_operator_b60_fixes}.* &#9670; ⛔ ***BUT THE SAME $\tfrac34$ SURVIVES TWICE IN \S`lift`, AND THERE IT IS LOAD-BEARING.*** *The lift's mode content is a normalizability computation on **the lift's own measure at the three hinges** --- a different locus and a different measure from the branch point --- and it rejects the growing branch by "the condition $\lambda<\tfrac34$ that no $\lambda=j+\tfrac12$ attains". ⇒ *The second occurrence makes it structural rather than incidental: the deck's third case is excluded because it needs the three sectors decoupled, **which is the lift's $\lambda<\tfrac34$ rejection read as a condition on the hinges --- "one inequality deciding both conjuncts, in opposite senses".*** So a wrong threshold there moves two conclusions at once and in opposite directions.* &#9670; ⚠ ***WHAT IS AND IS NOT AT RISK, BECAUSE THE MARGIN IS WORTH STATING WITH THE DEFECT.*** *The smallest admissible $\lambda$ is $j=\tfrac12$, giving $\lambda=1$, so **any threshold below $1$ returns the same verdict** and the conclusion has room. ⛔ *What is at risk is the NUMBER and the derivation behind it, and the corpus does not get to quote a threshold whose argument it has replaced elsewhere in the same paper --- nor to assume the branch-point measure carries to the hinges, which is the step that has never been run.* &#9670; ⛔⛭ ***STRUCK r6929 (66, on node 60's r6926): THE LIFT SUPPLIES ITS OWN MEASURE AND ITS OWN EXPONENT, AND THEY RETURN THE SAME THRESHOLD BY DIFFERENT ARITHMETIC.*** *The row asked whether the branch-point measure carries to the hinges. ⇒ ***It does not have to.*** **$\ell=(2\alpha/3)\sin(3\theta/2)$ is REGULAR at a hinge** --- it vanishes at $0,120,240^{\circ}$ with $\dd\ell/\dd\theta\to\alpha$ there, and is extremal at the wall angles, both verified --- **which is the opposite of the branch point's and means the measure cannot supply the threshold at all.** *It comes from the EXPONENT, and the exponent is a cube root because the time map is three-to-one*: on the lift $\tilde\tau$ is purely imaginary, so $\sinh^{2}(i\phi)=-\sin^{2}\phi$ and $\lvert r\rvert^{3}\propto\sin^{2}(3\theta/2)$, giving $\lvert r\rvert\sim\theta^{2/3}$ at a hinge --- **the same three that makes a hinge crossing a half-loop in $\ell$ and multiplies the mode by $\omega^{\lambda}$.** So the growing branch integrates $\theta^{-4\lambda/3}$, convergent iff $\lambda<\tfrac34$, confirmed by explicit integration at $\tfrac12$ (finite), $\tfrac34$ and $1$ (divergent).* &#9670; ⌗ ***SO THE TWO DERIVATIONS ARE INDEPENDENT AND THE ORDER'S CONDITIONAL NEVER FIRES.*** *The branch point gives the threshold from $2s+\tfrac12>-1$ and the lift from $-4\lambda/3>-1$: **two loci, two measures, one number.** The threshold does not differ from $\tfrac34$, so both conjuncts of \S`lift`'s deck argument hold at the same value and the one inequality deciding both in opposite senses is undisturbed; with $\lambda=j+\tfrac12$ and $j$ half-integer, $\lambda\in\{1,2,3,\dots\}$, so **the margin is $1$ against $\tfrac34$** exactly as the row recorded it. ⇒ *And the defect the row opened on was real: the count passage's derivation WAS removed and WAS the only one the paper carried. `P14` now states the lift's own measure and exponent beside the threshold, so it no longer rests on an argument the same paper has replaced.*\rcpt{P14_the_lifts_own_measure_returns_the_same_three_quarters_so_the_removed_derivation_was_not_the_only_one} &#9670; ***NO REMAINDER.*** *The row asked one question, it is answered, and the margin it flagged as the thing not at risk was not at risk.* &#9670; **WHAT WOULD DISCHARGE IT:** *the hinge normalizability recomputed on the current operator and the lift's own measure, reporting the threshold it gives; and, if it differs from $\tfrac34$, whether both conjuncts of \S`lift`'s deck argument still hold at the new value, since they are said to turn on one inequality in opposite senses.* ⌗ *Not asked: a new operator, a new lift, or a re-derivation of the count, which \S`count` already carries in its corrected form.* | ~~**STRUCK r6929** — opened r6921.~~ *No prior state: the branch-point half was corrected by the read and this half was named by it and not worked.* | P14 `sec:lift` |
| **PO-56** | **THE ACOUSTIC CONTRAST DIFFERENCE: THE PROJECTION'S GEOMETRY, ON THE CORPUS'S TWO-RATE ASSIGNMENT.** &#9670; ***OPENED r6891 (66) AS `PO-13`'s LIVE REMAINDER, ON THE STANDING ORDER.*** *`PO-13` was struck at `r6790` because the handover at the branch point supplies the one-locus state the row asked for, and it routed what it left --- the acoustic phase and the fourth peak --- into `P15` \S`sec:scope`'s prose rather than into a row. **Both of those have since dissolved** --- the phase intercept sits inside the sky's own locating spread and the fourth peak is $0.9$ to $1.3$ standard deviations out with most of the offset shared with the control --- **and what replaced them is a different object that has never had a row.*** &#9670; ⌗ ***AND WHERE THE LEAD CAME FROM, RECORDED AT `r6963` BECAUSE IT WAS ABOUT TO BE LOST: DARYL READ IT OFF THE GRAPH AS AN AMPLITUDE DIFFERENCE, BEFORE ANY OF THIS WAS MEASURED.*** *That reading is what sent the code seat after this sector in the first place --- **not a statistic, not a $\chi^{2}$ decomposition, a look at the two spectra plotted together** --- and the eliminations then ran for six revisions before arriving where it started. ⇒ *`cc66.46` measured the excess as symmetric about the envelope, $1.067$ on the peak side against $1.056$ on the trough side and $5.7$ per cent at the maxima against $5.3$ at the minima: **an amplitude excess carried by peaks and troughs both, which is the graph's own reading with numbers under it**.* ⌗ *It is recorded here for the same reason the withdrawn hypotheses are: the row's history is where the next reader learns what kind of evidence moved it, and **a correct lead taken from a figure is a different kind of evidence from a measurement and is worth being able to tell apart.** ⚠ *It licenses nothing on its own --- the amplitude reading is carried by `cc66.46`'s decomposition and not by the figure --- and the four hypotheses this chat seat lost along the way were all statistical.* &#9670; ***WHAT THE OBJECT IS, MEASURED RATHER THAN NAMED.*** *With $\Delta$ the whitened difference of the two arms at their own minima, $\chi^{2}$ decomposes exactly as $282.96=177.88+27.82+77.26$, so the model difference carries three quarters of the excess and the rejection is not one sky's luck: this arm scores $1.43$ per bin against the control's $0.99$ on a typical sky. $\Delta$ is **trough-dominated** ($+0.011$ at the peaks against $-0.760$ at the troughs), its oscillatory part carries seven tenths of it, and that part is antisymmetric about the **envelope** rather than about each peak --- **a contrast, and not a phase and not an envelope**. This arm's acoustic oscillation is $1.040$ times the control's about its own envelope.* &#9670; ***WHAT IS ALREADY RULED OUT, CARRIED ACROSS SO THE ROW STARTS WHERE THE WORK ENDED.*** *The handover amplitude, **exactly** --- it is absorbed by the one fitted amplitude. The neutrino hierarchy's truncation depth ($93.7\%$ of $\lVert\Delta\rVert^{2}$ survives it), the early integrated Sachs--Wolfe term ($92.8\%$), and the continuity half of the driving ($87.6\%$). The Euler half reaches $68.3\%$ but at a direction cosine of $-0.16$, which is not the direction. And the whole amplitude--tilt--damping--position family together carries $5.73\%$, against $51\%$ for a single control-only direction that raises the oscillation at fixed envelope with nothing fitted.* &#9670; ***THE CHANNEL IS IDENTIFIED AND THE CAUSE IS NOT.*** *Removing the monopole and removing the Doppler term bracket the contrast direction with **opposite signs**, on the line-of-sight path and on the reporting path alike and with the same signs on both, so the reading is not an artefact of the path it first had to be measured on. **But neither deletion returns the two arms to a common contrast** ($1.0376$ and $1.0818$ against the base $1.0401$), so the bracket locates where the difference enters and not what produces it. About a third of $\lVert\Delta\rVert^{2}$ is unnamed.* &#9670; ⚑ ***THE ONE REACHABLE CANDIDATE HAS NOW BEEN POINTED AT THE QUESTION, AND IT IS RULED OUT --- `r6893`.*** *The free-streaming knob is located sub-bin by a parabola vertex and again by an envelope-normalised cross-correlation, on both arms and again at four times the multipole sampling.* ⚠ **The shift is NOT uniform, which CORRECTS `r6889`**: band by band it rises monotonically, $+2.9/+5.6/+9.4/+10.9/+12.6$ on the control against $+3.1/+5.7/+9.3/+10.9/+12.5$ on the arm --- *the uniformity `r6889` gated was the grid, and its tolerance was $0.05$ on integers that could only differ by a whole bin.* ⇒ ***AND IT IS COMMON TO THE TWO ARMS: the largest band-by-band difference is $0.17$ of a multipole and the extremum-averaged shift in units of each arm's own $\ell_A$ agrees to $1.7\times10^{-4}$*** --- so on this seat's own criterion, *a phase shift common to both is not a candidate for $\Delta$ and one that differs between them is*, **it is not a candidate**. ⌗ *And the drag half, kept apart from the phase and split in two because it is two things, says the same independently: removing free-streaming raises the **envelope** by $25.7\%$ and changes the peak-to-trough **contrast** by $-1.3\%$, on both arms, agreeing to $0.0004$ and $0.0006$ --- and $\Delta$ is a CONTRAST difference, so the knob's large effect is in the wrong quantity.* ⇒ *In $\Delta$'s own space the two arms' `NUFS` directions sit at a whitened cosine of $0.99918$; their difference freely rescaled could reach $8.7\%$ of $\lVert\Delta\rVert^{2}$ at a **negative** coefficient, and there is no such freedom, because both arms carry the same neutrino sector.* &#9670; ⚑⚑ ***AND THE HAZARD THIS ROW CARRIED IS DISCHARGED --- `r6893`, by an accounting and not by a fourth find.*** *Three switches had been found verified on a path they reach and used on a path they do not --- the tilt literal, the baryon density, and the two source terms --- and three by three routes is a **rate**, not a count.* ⇒ **The sweep enumerates all SIXTY environment switches the instrument reads, records from the source text which of the three source constructions and which of the two solver paths reads each, and measures fifty-five of them on both arms one switch at a time.** *Fifty-one have a live use site on the reporting path; the nine that do not come back **bit-identical** there --- all eighteen runs at exactly $0.0$ --- and again at full $\ell$ reach, where `DAMPX` and `RD` would have shown if they touched the damping tail.* ⇒ ***The set of bit-identical runs is EXACTLY the set four readings predict --- off-path, rate-identity on the control arm, the arm branch, and gated by another switch --- with no unexplained null and nothing explained away that in fact moved.*** &#9670; ⛭ ***TWO THINGS THE SWEEP DID TURN UP, neither of the `r6476` class and neither moving a reported number.*** *(i) `LRSFROM` moves the instrument's reported $r_s$ by a quarter, $145.38\to110.49$ Mpc, and its spectrum by **exactly zero**: $R_S$ and $\ell_A$ are **diagnostics**, reaching a `print` and the `SAVE` metadata and nothing else, and `hier_run` accepts $\ell_A$, $D_M$ and $r_s$ and uses none of the three --- so `r6476`'s reachability check for that switch was taken on the **header**, and the header is not the reported number.* ⇒ *And the consequence points the useful way: because no transfer function reads $\ell_A$, the printed $\ell_1/\ell_A=0.7312$ agreeing with the sky's $220.6/301.7$ is an agreement between **two independently computed quantities** and not a value fed in.* *(ii) `LATARG`, which the instrument calls the corpus's one fitted number, has **no root** at the arm's adjudicated background --- the onset solve raises across the whole widened bracket --- which is why the refit command supplies `ZSTART=3e7`, so nothing reported moves and the register had not said the switch is unreachable there.* &#9670; **WHAT WOULD DISCHARGE IT:** *a mechanism that raises this arm's acoustic oscillation about its own envelope by four per cent where the control's is not raised --- or a demonstration that the monopole-to-Doppler balance cannot differ between the arms at that size, which would move the search to the unnamed third.* &#9670; ⚠ ***AND TWO CONFIRMATIONS ARE OWED, RECORDED HERE BECAUSE THEY ARE NOT ON THE TREE r6895 (66, gating).*** *The locator's primary run is on the banked spectra and passes; the `LSTEP=2` re-run at four times the multipole sampling --- the test that the non-uniformity is not a property of the grid --- and the full-reach re-run of the nine off-path nulls both read banks that are not yet pushed, and the receipt fails on their absence rather than reporting the parts it can run as the whole. **What stands without them**: the shift's sign, its non-uniformity (which is also established statically, the superseded gate's tolerance being $0.05$ on bin-centre differences whose smallest non-zero spread is $5$), and the arms' agreement --- that last being a difference taken between two runs on one grid with one locator, which a grid property moves alike and so cannot manufacture. **What does not**: the shape of the rise with multipole, which is exactly what the finer grid is there to settle.* &#9670; ⚠ ***THE TWO BANKS LANDED AND A THIRD DEPENDENCY APPEARED WITH THEM, FOR A SUBSTANTIVE REASON r6897 (66, gating).*** *`r6893_fine_grid_*` and `r6893_full_reach_nulls_*` are on the tree and the parts reading them pass. **But the finer grid's high-multipole half is summed over wavenumber slices**, and whether that summation is valid at the second stage's own slicing and reach is a further measurement, banked separately and not yet pushed --- so the receipt still fails on one bank and the grid-independence of the rise is still not established. ⇒ *That is the code seat setting its own dependency rather than declaring the confirmation done, and the hold on the shape stands unchanged.* ⌗ **What did move**: the amplitude's own number, an envelope rescale of about a quarter rather than the third the earlier whole-spectrum move suggested, which is banked and is in the paper.* &#9670; ⛭ ***THE LOADING READING IS REFUTED BY SIGN, AND THE RESIDUAL IS LARGER THAN IT LOOKED --- r6909 (66, on the code seat's r6897+cc66.39).*** *This seat's order proposed that the contrast excess and the alternation excess were one displacement of the oscillation's zero point, and enumerated two outcomes. **The answer is a third.** Both arms' offsets approach the tight-coupling equilibrium $-R\Psi$ from below, reaching $0.78$ of it on the control and $0.76$ on the arm --- so the absolute question answers no on both and by nearly the same amount, which is the approach and not a property of either arm. ⇒ *Calibrated against a known change in loading --- the control at $\omega_b\pm8$ per cent, the estimator tracking $77$ per cent of it --- **the arm's offset falls short of what its own loading accounts for by an effective $\omega_b$ of $-2.9$ per cent.** Too much loading is what deepens troughs; this arm's monopole behaves as though it had too little.* &#9670; ⛔ ***AND THE ORDER'S PREMISE WAS WRONG, WHICH IS THE PART THIS SEAT OWNS.*** *It read the alternation excess against the **sky** and the contrast against the **control** and treated them as pointing the same way. **Against the control the arm's $P_1/P_2$ is $2.141$ against $2.191$ --- lower.** The two were never aligned.* &#9670; ⚭ ***AND THE REFRAMING, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE ELIMINATION.*** *Three channels that would each **shallow** the troughs are all larger on this arm: the monopole's offset falls short of its own loading; **WHAT WOULD DISCHARGE IT, RESTATED ON THAT:** *a mechanism in the **generation** of the acoustic oscillation relative to its own envelope, large enough to clear three trough-filling channels that all run the other way. ⚠ *The driving is not obviously it either*: the measured grip is $0.1717$ against the control's $0.1792$ --- weaker, and weaker driving makes less oscillation. **Four channels now point the wrong way, and that is the sharpest thing the sector has.*** &#9670; ⛔ ***AND THIS SEAT'S NAMED ROUTE IS OUT TWICE OVER, THE FIRST HALF BEING A PREMISE CORRECTION.*** *The order's route was the arms' distances differing by six per cent, $13005$ against $13865$~Mpc. **That pair is the superseded configuration's** ($r_s=135.46/144.53$), where this arm's was the smaller; The cancellation the route relies on is real --- $\ell_A$ agree to $0.085$ per cent --- so it is the six per cent and not the reasoning that fails.* ⇒ *And the route is eliminated by measurement anyway: projecting either arm's own source through the other's distance moves its contrast by under a per cent, and **removing the difference altogether takes the ratio UP**, $1.045\to1.051$.* &#9670; ⌗ ***WHERE IN THE PROJECTION, AND NO FURTHER.*** *This arm's projection retains $1.054$ times as much of its own source oscillation as the control's does; the excess **rises** with multipole where the source ratio is flat; **WHAT WOULD DISCHARGE IT, RESTATED AGAIN:** *what within the projection raises this arm's contrast by five per cent when its source oscillation is if anything shallower --- or a demonstration that the projection cannot differ between two arms whose acoustic angles agree to a part in a thousand, which would make the five per cent an instrument fact rather than a physical one and is the first thing to rule out.* &#9670; ⛑⛑ ***THE CROSS TERM IS EXCLUDED AND THE EXCESS IS IN EVERY PROJECTED TERM AT ONCE --- r6915+cc66.41.*** *This seat asked which term carries it after projection and named the monopole--Doppler pair first, $j_\ell$ and $j_\ell'$ being a quarter period out of phase.* ⛔ ***And the closure gate this seat forced had to be corrected before it could be passed:*** *the **transfer** is linear in the source and the pieces do add there, but $C_\ell$ is **quadratic** in the transfer, so the projected **spectra** cannot --- the four diagonal pieces alone fall short of $D_\ell$ by up to $46$ per cent. ⌗ *That is not the third outcome this seat named: the **first** presupposes a cross term, so the two clauses were in tension and the first was the coherent one.* ⇒ **What closes is the full bilinear decomposition, ten pair spectra summing to $D_\ell$ to $1.1$ and $1.3\times10^{-15}$ relative on the two arms.** &#9670; ⛔ ***THE CROSS TERM IS NOT THE CHANNEL:*** *deleting it moves the ratio from $1.0452$ to $1.0451$, its carry running $-0.0013$ to $+0.0008$ of the $+0.045$ over four envelope windows --- **bounded at about a part in a thousand**.* &#9670; ***AND THE LADDER IS THE SHARPEST FORM OF IT*** --- *every term's **source** ratio is at or below $0.992$ and every term's **projected** ratio is higher: monopole $0.992\to1.034$, Doppler $0.973\to1.099$, ISW $0.972\to0.989$, polarisation $0.990\to1.058$.* &#9670; ⌗ ***THE SPLIT THIS SEAT'S REASONING WAS AFTER IS ABOUT $40/60$, THE RESPONSE THE LARGER.*** *The weight **does** differ --- the arm's monopole share $0.5225$ against $0.5092$, its Doppler $0.1753$ against $0.1840$ --- and rebuilding the arm's pieces at the control's shares gives $1.0267$, so the weights carry $+0.019$ of the $+0.045$ and each piece's own response $+0.027$; the reverse construction agrees at $+0.020$.* &#9670; ⛑ ***AND THE RETAINED FRACTION IS NOT FLAT IN $q$***, *which is the branch this seat named as the kernel-phase signature: it rises from $1.030$ in the lowest band to $1.089$ in the highest, slope $+0.0139\pm0.0021$ per unit $q$ over twelve settings and **never once negative** --- so the branch that would have killed the reading is excluded. ⌗ The intercept is $1.017\pm0.010$ and **straddles one**, so the rise is established and a constant offset is not.*\rcpt{P15_the_cross_term_is_not_the_channel_and_every_projected_term_carries_the_excess} &#9670; ⛔ ***AND r6919 (66) TAKES THE TWO CORRECTIONS cc66.41 MADE TO ITS ORDER, BOTH OF THEM THIS SEAT'S.*** *The order's closure gate said the separately projected pieces must sum to the full spectrum. **The TRANSFER is linear and the pieces add there;
